# Chord-economy field census (compiler-derived)

Generated by `scripts/Chord-Field-Census.ps1` from `scripts/chord_field_census.lean.template`
(round BB, 2026-09-28; doc 110 item R5 step 1). Regenerate with
`pwsh -File scripts/Chord-Field-Census.ps1` after the library is built; the script never builds.

- Generated: 2026-10-08 07:48 from commit `ac5be00` (10 uncommitted paths in the working tree; the census reads the oleans on disk)
- `lake env lean --version`: `Lean (version 4.29.1, x86_64-w64-windows-gnu, commit f72c35b3f637c8c6571d353742168ab66cc22c00, Release)`
- Imports: `Sanctions`; elaboration 143 s at LEAN_NUM_THREADS=3
- Theorems: the default set, every row of the `HEADLINE-SNAPSHOT.md` headline table in the `ChordEconomy` / `ChordEconomyTwoSided` / `ChordEconomyRelief` / `ChordEconomyCs` namespaces (19 rows)
- Censused: 57 own fields of `ChordEconomy` + 8 of `ChordEconomyTwoSided` + 11 of `ChordEconomyRelief` + 3 of `ChordEconomyCs` = 79 class fields, and the 18 fields of the inherited `AbstractGame.Params`, counted separately

## What is measured

For each theorem the census walks, breadth-first, every constant of the `Sanctions` library reachable from
the theorem through types **and** values (statement and proof), and records every field whose projection
function, or a raw `Expr.proj` at its index, occurs in a visited constant. Two readings:

- **Direct**: projection functions are recorded and not expanded. A field is direct when some constant in the closure mentions it.
- **Closed**: projection functions are also expanded through their types, so a field whose own statement mentions another field pulls that one in (for example `hUS_crossHi` mentions `rhi`). Closed is the smallest field set closed under the structures' own dependency order: what a tier structure holding the theorem would have to contain. Closed-only fields are marked `°` below.

Scope. It is a constant-level census, not a data-flow one: a field counts when any constant in the closure mentions
its projection. For the class fields the only objects in play are the theorem's own economies (a theorem over two,
such as `ChordEconomyCs.exists_defiance_equilibrium_mono_βT`, is censused as their union); for the `Params` fields
it over-approximates wherever a lemma over an arbitrary `Params`
is applied to a derived one. It is exact for the proofs as they stand and not minimal over all proofs: a lemma that
proves more than the theorem needs still contributes every field its own proof reads. The `Params` counts are fields
*read*, not fields a binder's type presupposes: an equilibrium record `FeedbackStackelbergEquilibriumTop p` needs a
whole `p : Params` to be stated, but only the fields some constant in the closure projects are counted.

Two kinds of whole-structure use are reported separately:

- An **eliminator** use of a censused structure (`casesOn`, `rec`, `recOn`, `noConfusion`) reads fields positionally,
  invisible to a projection census. A row with an entry in the *Eliminators* column has a census that is a lower bound.
- A **constructor** use (`mk`) builds a new instance, typically by a structure update `{ p with β_S := … }`, whose
  elaborated form mentions every field it does not replace. A field every one of whose readers is such a builder
  is marked `†` (**copy-only**): the closure shows it moved into a new instance and nowhere used. It is still counted,
  since the closure cannot see whether the copy's field is later read, but the *excl. †* columns leave it out.

The *text bound* column is the R5 verifier's comment-stripped name-scan closure (doc 110 R5, V.source_check (4)),
kept as reference data in the script.
